Yeah, um… I came in because it’s gotten kind of noticeable, I guess. Like people can see something’s off with how I’m moving. Sometimes I’m moving so slow it feels like I’m underwater—like I take forever to answer someone or even just get out of a chair.

And then other times I’m the opposite, like I’m pacing around my apartment and rubbing my hands together, like I’m trying to shake off a really bad call from work. I do customer service, so bad calls happen, but… this feels different. Sorry, I’m kind of circling around it. It’s mostly that people can actually see it now.

Um… I’m not totally sure what counts as “organized.” Like, if you mean can I follow a conversation, mostly yeah, I think so.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think so… I mean, my sleep is kind of all over the place sometimes, but feeling rested on less sleep? Not really something I’ve noticed.

Like, if I only sleep a few hours, I usually just feel tired and kind of useless the next day. I wish I could get by on less sleep, honestly—my neighbor upstairs seems to vacuum at the worst times, which is… not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I get that “I barely slept and I’m totally fine” thing.

Yeah… I mean, I’m not completely falling apart or anything. I can still pay rent, keep food around, you know, keep a roof over my head. But it does mess with things.

Work is the main one. I’ve kind of lost momentum with steady jobs because I miss deadlines, or I avoid calls when I’m supposed to be on them, and then people start treating me like I’m unreliable. Which… I guess I am, in those moments. It’s frustrating because customer service is already one of those jobs where one missed thing turns into three, and then you’re thinking about the whole queue and the little timer and—sorry, that’s not exactly the point.

Relationships too, I guess. I see friends less. I don’t really make plans unless someone pushes me. And basic stuff slides, like meals, errands, even hiking, which I used to actually like. I can do it if I force myself, but it takes a lot more pushing than it should.

It eases up briefly, then comes back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had days where I’m in a better mood, like if a customer actually says thank you or my supervisor isn’t on my case, but I wouldn’t call it extremely happy or anything. \n\nI do get irritated sometimes, but… I think that’s kind of normal? delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: I’ve had stretches of a few weeks where my mood was closer to normal and I was getting things done, but I still heard the supervisor voice or felt sure the old ticketing software was flagging me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I can still keep a roof over my head, but I’ve lost momentum with steady work because I miss deadlines, avoid calls, and come across as unreliable. I also see friends less and let basic routines like meals, errands, and hiking slide unless I force myself.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 years and 3 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 2 years and 3 months, starting after I was fired from my customer-support job.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 8 months\n  manifestation: The voices and tracking beliefs have been around for about a year and 8 months, not just during the worst sadness.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 2 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The depression side has been present for about 2 years and 1 month, with only brief periods where it eases up.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 30 / customer_service_representative / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S028\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that weren't really there?\n\nPatient: Seen things? No, not really. I don’t think I’ve seen anything that wasn’t there.\n\nIt’s more like hearing stuff, like that old headset beep from work, or a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” and then I check and my laptop’s closed, there’s no phone ringing. But visually, no… I can’t think of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found that you can get by with much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, my sleep is kind of all over the place sometimes, but feeling rested on less sleep? Not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nLike, if I only sleep a few hours, I usually just feel tired and kind of useless the next day. I wish I could get by on less sleep, honestly—my neighbor upstairs seems to vacuum at the worst times, which is… not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I get that “I barely slept and I’m totally fine” thing.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not completely falling apart or anything. I can still pay rent, keep food around, you know, keep a roof over my head. But it does mess with things.\n\nWork is the main one. I’ve kind of lost momentum with steady jobs because I miss deadlines, or I avoid calls when I’m supposed to be on them, and then people start treating me like I’m unreliable. Which… I guess I am, in those moments. It’s frustrating because customer service is already one of those jobs where one missed thing turns into three, and then you’re thinking about the whole queue and the little timer and—sorry, that’s not exactly the point.\n\nRelationships too, I guess. I see friends less. I don’t really make plans unless someone pushes me. And basic stuff slides, like meals, errands, even hiking, which I used to actually like. I can do it if I force myself, but it takes a lot more pushing than it should.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I decided to stop liking things, it’s more like they just don’t… catch.

I used to sketch a lot, and I’d keep these little coffee notes, like which beans tasted kind of chocolatey or burnt or whatever. And weekend hikes used to make me feel like myself. Lately I’ll open the notebook, or I’ll put my boots by the door, and I just kind of sit there. There’s nothing pulling me toward it.

Which is annoying, because the boots are right there, and I even bought new socks for hiking once—too expensive, honestly—but then I don’t go. It’s like the idea of it is still familiar, but the feeling isn’t really there.

It’s not like just a bad week or something. It eases up briefly, then comes back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had days where I’m in a better mood, like if a customer actually says thank you or my supervisor isn’t on my case, but I wouldn’t call it extremely happy or anything. \n\nI do get irritated sometimes, but… I think that’s kind of normal? The energy part is harder—I don’t think I’ve had that “wired and angry” thing for a whole stretch of time.\n\nSometimes after a bad call I’ll be kind of keyed up, but then I just sort of crash or sit there thinking about it too long. So… no, I don’t think so. Not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or extremely irritable and had a lot of energy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not for several days. I mean, I’ve had times where I’m annoyed or I get a little more talkative if something good happens, like if a call goes smoothly or I get out of work early, but that’s not really days of, like, big energy.\n\nHonestly I’m usually just trying to get through the day. My sleep gets weird sometimes depending on my schedule—well, and my upstairs neighbor stomps around, so that doesn’t help—but no, I can’t really point to a stretch where I was extremely happy or extremely irritable and full of energy for days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Extremely happy with a lot of energy… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had good moods, sure, like if the day goes better than expected, or if I actually get through my errands without something weird happening—like last week the grocery store was out of the coffee I like and that kind of threw me off, but that’s not what you’re asking.\n\nSo, yeah… I wouldn’t say I’ve had a period like that. Nothing where I felt unusually happy and energized in some big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood changes are related to your menstrual cycle?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m male, so I don’t have a menstrual cycle. I don’t think so. Not like going out and doing dangerous stuff or making big sudden decisions. It’s… not loud like someone yelling right next to me, but it feels real enough that I check.\n\nAnd then I get annoyed at myself because I’ll be like, okay, why am I still hearing that when I’m off the clock? Sorry, that’s probably not the point—yes, I’ve heard things that weren’t really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure what counts as “organized.” Like, if you mean can I follow a conversation, mostly yeah, I think so.\n\nSometimes I lose my place if I’m trying to explain something and then I start thinking about three examples at once—kind of like now, honestly. I’m not sure… I mean, I get irritated, sure, but I don’t know if it’s that kind of “extremely” irritated thing you mean.

I mean, I’ve had times where I’m annoyed or I get a little more talkative if something good happens, like if a call goes smoothly or I get out of work early, but that’s not really days of, like, big energy.\n\nHonestly I’m usually just trying to get through the day. My sleep gets weird sometimes depending on my schedule—well, and my upstairs neighbor stomps around, so that doesn’t help—but no, I can’t really point to a stretch where I was extremely happy or extremely irritable and full of energy for days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Extremely happy with a lot of energy… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had good moods, sure, like if the day goes better than expected, or if I actually get through my errands without something weird happening—like last week the grocery store was out of the coffee I like and that kind of threw me off, but that’s not what you’re asking.\n\nSo, yeah… I wouldn’t say I’ve had a period like that. Nothing where I felt unusually happy and energized in some big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood changes are related to your menstrual cycle?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m male, so I don’t have a menstrual cycle. Unless you mean like some kind of monthly pattern in general? I haven’t really tracked it that way.\n\nI do notice stuff lines up more with work stress or sleep being off, or just random things during the week… like if the schedule changes, that can mess with me. But menstrual cycle, no, that doesn’t really apply to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely irritable and had reckless or impulsive behavior?\n\nPatient: I mean… I get irritable sometimes, yeah, like with customers repeating the same complaint or when the phone system freezes right in the middle of a call, and then I’m just sitting there listening to hold music like I don’t already hear it all day.\n\nBut reckless or impulsive? I don’t think so. Not like going out and doing dangerous stuff or making big sudden decisions. I might snap a little or want to be left alone, but I can’t think of a period where I was extremely irritable and then acting reckless because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that weren't really there?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like seeing a person standing there or anything. But I do hear things sometimes.\n\nIt’s usually work-type stuff, which is weird because I’m not even working at that moment. Like I’ll hear the old headset beep, or a supervisor kind of voice saying my name, or something like “too slow,” and then I look over and my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing. It’s… not loud like someone yelling right next to me, but it feels real enough that I check.\n\nAnd then I get annoyed at myself because I’ll be like, okay, why am I still hearing that when I’m off the clock? The headset we used to have made this specific beep, too, and I hated it. Sorry, that’s probably not the point—yes, I’ve heard things that weren’t really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure what counts as “organized.” Like, if you mean can I follow a conversation, mostly yeah, I think so.\n\nSometimes I lose my place if I’m trying to explain something and then I start thinking about three examples at once—kind of like now, honestly. At work they want notes in a certain format, and I’ll get stuck deciding what part goes first, but that might just be normal because the software is annoying and there are all these little boxes.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say my thoughts feel completely scrambled, but I do get a little scattered sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that weren't really there?\n\nPatient: Seen things? No, not really. I don’t think I’ve seen anything that wasn’t there.\n\nIt’s more like hearing stuff, like that old headset beep from work, or a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” and then I check and my laptop’s closed, there’s no phone ringing. But visually, no… I can’t think of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found that you can get by with much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, my sleep is kind of all over the place sometimes, but feeling rested on less sleep? Not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nLike, if I only sleep a few hours, I usually just feel tired and kind of useless the next day. I wish I could get by on less sleep, honestly—my neighbor upstairs seems to vacuum at the worst times, which is… not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I get that “I barely slept and I’m totally fine” thing.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not completely falling apart or anything. I can still pay rent, keep food around, you know, keep a roof over my head. But it does mess with things.\n\nWork is the main one. I’ve kind of lost momentum with steady jobs because I miss deadlines, or I avoid calls when I’m supposed to be on them, and then people start treating me like I’m unreliable. I see friends less. I don’t really make plans unless someone pushes me. And basic stuff slides, like meals, errands, even hiking, which I used to actually like. I can do it if I force myself, but it takes a lot more pushing than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Yeah… sometimes it’s more noticeable than other times. Like I’ll hear the old headset beep, that little work call sound, even when my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing or anything.

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I do. Probably more than I want to admit.

It’s usually over small stuff, like at work if I reply too slowly or I miss some little detail, I’ll keep replaying it after. Or with contract jobs I’ve done, I’ll think, okay, that one message took too long, so I must’ve let everyone down. And then my brain goes from that to, like, well, maybe I deserved to lose the job or get fired.

Which sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, because sometimes it’s just one email or one customer ticket, but it doesn’t feel small in my head. It turns into this whole… proof that I’m not really good enough.

I don’t think so. Not like going out and doing dangerous stuff or making big sudden decisions. I might snap a little or want to be left alone, but I can’t think of a period where I was extremely irritable and then acting reckless because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that weren't really there?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like seeing a person standing there or anything. But I do hear things sometimes.\n\nIt’s usually work-type stuff, which is weird because I’m not even working at that moment. Like I’ll hear the old headset beep, or a supervisor kind of voice saying my name, or something like “too slow,” and then I look over and my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing. It’s… not loud like someone yelling right next to me, but it feels real enough that I check.\n\nAnd then I get annoyed at myself because I’ll be like, okay, why am I still hearing that when I’m off the clock? The headset we used to have made this specific beep, too, and I hated it. Sorry, that’s probably not the point—yes, I’ve heard things that weren’t really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure what counts as “organized.” Like, if you mean can I follow a conversation, mostly yeah, I think so.\n\nSometimes I lose my place if I’m trying to explain something and then I start thinking about three examples at once—kind of like now, honestly. At work they want notes in a certain format, and I’ll get stuck deciding what part goes first, but that might just be normal because the software is annoying and there are all these little boxes.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say my thoughts feel completely scrambled, but I do get a little scattered sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that weren't really there?\n\nPatient: Seen things? I don’t think I’ve seen anything that wasn’t there.\n\nIt’s more like hearing stuff, like that old headset beep from work, or a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” and then I check and my laptop’s closed, there’s no phone ringing. But menstrual cycle, no, that doesn’t really apply to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely irritable and had reckless or impulsive behavior?\n\nPatient: I mean… I get irritable sometimes, yeah, like with customers repeating the same complaint or when the phone system freezes right in the middle of a call, and then I’m just sitting there listening to hold music like I don’t already hear it all day.\n\nBut reckless or impulsive? I don’t think so. Not like going out and doing dangerous stuff or making big sudden decisions. I might snap a little or want to be left alone, but I can’t think of a period where I was extremely irritable and then acting reckless because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that weren't really there?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like seeing a person standing there or anything. But I do hear things sometimes.\n\nIt’s usually work-type stuff, which is weird because I’m not even working at that moment. Like I’ll hear the old headset beep, or a supervisor kind of voice saying my name, or something like “too slow,” and then I look over and my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing. It’s… not loud like someone yelling right next to me, but it feels real enough that I check.\n\nAnd then I get annoyed at myself because I’ll be like, okay, why am I still hearing that when I’m off the clock? The headset we used to have made this specific beep, too, and I hated it. Sorry, that’s probably not the point—yes, I’ve heard things that weren’t really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure what counts as “organized.” Like, if you mean can I follow a conversation, mostly yeah, I think so.\n\nSometimes I lose my place if I’m trying to explain something and then I start thinking about three examples at once—kind of like now, honestly. At work they want notes in a certain format, and I’ll get stuck deciding what part goes first, but that might just be normal because the software is annoying and there are all these little boxes.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say my thoughts feel completely scrambled, but I do get a little scattered sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that weren't really there?\n\nPatient: Seen things? No, not really. I don’t think I’ve seen anything that wasn’t there.\n\nIt’s more like hearing stuff, like that old headset beep from work, or a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” and then I check and my laptop’s closed, there’s no phone ringing. But visually, no… I can’t think of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found that you can get by with much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, my sleep is kind of all over the place sometimes, but feeling rested on less sleep? Not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nLike, if I only sleep a few hours, I usually just feel tired and kind of useless the next day. I wish I could get by on less sleep, honestly—my neighbor upstairs seems to vacuum at the worst times, which is… not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I get that “I barely slept and I’m totally fine” thing.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not completely falling apart or anything. I can still pay rent, keep food around, you know, keep a roof over my head. But it does mess with things.\n\nWork is the main one. I’ve kind of lost momentum with steady jobs because I miss deadlines, or I avoid calls when I’m supposed to be on them, and then people start treating me like I’m unreliable. Which… I guess I am, in those moments. It’s frustrating because customer service is already one of those jobs where one missed thing turns into three, and then you’re thinking about the whole queue and the little timer and—sorry, that’s not exactly the point.\n\nRelationships too, I guess. I see friends less. I don’t really make plans unless someone pushes me. And basic stuff slides, like meals, errands, even hiking, which I used to actually like. I can do it if I force myself, but it takes a lot more pushing than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I decided to stop liking things, it’s more like they just don’t… catch.\n\nI used to sketch a lot, and I’d keep these little coffee notes, like which beans tasted kind of chocolatey or burnt or whatever. And weekend hikes used to make me feel like myself. Lately I’ll open the notebook, or I’ll put my boots by the door, and I just kind of sit there. There’s nothing pulling me toward it.\n\nWhich is annoying, because the boots are right there, and I even bought new socks for hiking once—too expensive, honestly—but then I don’t go. It’s like the idea of it is still familiar, but the feeling isn’t really there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I get irritated, sure, but I don’t know if it’s that kind of “extremely” irritated thing you mean.\n\nAnd happy… not really in a big way. My thoughts can feel busy sometimes, like I’ll be thinking about a bill, then a work message, then whether I forgot laundry in the washer — which I actually did once and had to rewash everything because it got that smell — but I don’t know if I’d call it racing.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your hallucinations, such as hearing voices, were particularly vivid or disturbing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it’s more noticeable than other times. Customer service timers are honestly awful—sorry, I know that’s not the point.\n\nSo, yeah, it can be disturbing, mostly because it feels so specific. Like it’s using work stuff against me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're worthless or have a low opinion of yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. But visually, no… I can’t think of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found that you can get by with much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, my sleep is kind of all over the place sometimes, but feeling rested on less sleep? Not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nLike, if I only sleep a few hours, I usually just feel tired and kind of useless the next day. I wish I could get by on less sleep, honestly—my neighbor upstairs seems to vacuum at the worst times, which is… not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I get that “I barely slept and I’m totally fine” thing.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not completely falling apart or anything. I can still pay rent, keep food around, you know, keep a roof over my head. But it does mess with things.\n\nWork is the main one. I’ve kind of lost momentum with steady jobs because I miss deadlines, or I avoid calls when I’m supposed to be on them, and then people start treating me like I’m unreliable. Which… I guess I am, in those moments. It’s frustrating because customer service is already one of those jobs where one missed thing turns into three, and then you’re thinking about the whole queue and the little timer and—sorry, that’s not exactly the point.\n\nRelationships too, I guess. I see friends less. I don’t really make plans unless someone pushes me. And basic stuff slides, like meals, errands, even hiking, which I used to actually like. I can do it if I force myself, but it takes a lot more pushing than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I decided to stop liking things, it’s more like they just don’t… catch.\n\nI used to sketch a lot, and I’d keep these little coffee notes, like which beans tasted kind of chocolatey or burnt or whatever. And weekend hikes used to make me feel like myself. Lately I’ll open the notebook, or I’ll put my boots by the door, and I just kind of sit there. There’s nothing pulling me toward it.\n\nWhich is annoying, because the boots are right there, and I even bought new socks for hiking once—too expensive, honestly—but then I don’t go. It’s like the idea of it is still familiar, but the feeling isn’t really there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I get irritated, sure, but I don’t know if it’s that kind of “extremely” irritated thing you mean.\n\nAnd happy… not really in a big way. My thoughts can feel busy sometimes, like I’ll be thinking about a bill, then a work message, then whether I forgot laundry in the washer — which I actually did once and had to rewash everything because it got that smell — but I don’t know if I’d call it racing.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your hallucinations, such as hearing voices, were particularly vivid or disturbing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it’s more noticeable than other times. It turns into this whole… proof that I’m not really good enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or extremely irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not for several days in a row.\n\nI mean, I can get irritable, like if something keeps beeping or there’s some customer email that’s worded in that passive-aggressive way—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But it doesn’t feel like this big several-day stretch where I’m just extremely happy or angry the whole time.\n\nExtremely happy, definitely not that I can remember. More like normal little moments, if anything. So… no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I get annoyed in the moment—like if there’s a weird work message or I’m already tired and something stupid happens with the system—but it usually doesn’t last days.\n\nI might complain about it too much, honestly. Like I’ll tell myself I’m just being “efficient” by noticing all the broken processes, but sometimes I’m just irritated. But several days in a row where I’m extremely irritable? I don’t think so. Nothing that stands out like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period where your hallucinations were so real that you acted on them as if they were real?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 30 / customer_service_representative / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"I’ll hear the old headset beep or a supervisor-type voice say my name or 'too slow' when my laptop is closed and there’s no phone ringing.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Kind of… I mean, I’ve definitely reacted to it at first like it was real.

Like I’ll hear the old headset beep, the one from work, or a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” even when my laptop is closed and there’s no phone ringing. And I’ll sort of look around or check the laptop like, wait, did I miss something?

It’s weird because my brain goes into work mode immediately. Customer service stuff does that anyway—those little alert sounds get burned into your head. But then I realize, no, nothing’s actually on, so… I don’t know if that counts as “acting on it,” but I have responded to it like it was happening for a moment.

I do customer service, so bad calls happen, but… this feels different. Sorry, I’m kind of circling around it. It eases up briefly, then comes back.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had days where I’m in a better mood, like if a customer actually says thank you or my supervisor isn’t on my case, but I wouldn’t call it extremely happy or anything. \n\nI do get irritated sometimes, but… I think that’s kind of normal? So… no, I don’t think so. My sleep gets weird sometimes depending on my schedule—well, and my upstairs neighbor stomps around, so that doesn’t help—but no, I can’t really point to a stretch where I was extremely happy or extremely irritable and full of energy for days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy and had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Extremely happy with a lot of energy… no, I don’t think so. But I do hear things sometimes.\n\nIt’s usually work-type stuff, which is weird because I’m not even working at that moment. Like I’ll hear the old headset beep, or a supervisor kind of voice saying my name, or something like “too slow,” and then I look over and my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing. It’s… not loud like someone yelling right next to me, but it feels real enough that I check.\n\nAnd then I get annoyed at myself because I’ll be like, okay, why am I still hearing that when I’m off the clock? The headset we used to have made this specific beep, too, and I hated it. Sorry, that’s probably not the point—yes, I’ve heard things that weren’t really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure what counts as “organized.” Like, if you mean can I follow a conversation, mostly yeah, I think so.\n\nSometimes I lose my place if I’m trying to explain something and then I start thinking about three examples at once—kind of like now, honestly. At work they want notes in a certain format, and I’ll get stuck deciding what part goes first, but that might just be normal because the software is annoying and there are all these little boxes.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say my thoughts feel completely scrambled, but I do get a little scattered sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that weren't really there?\n\nPatient: Seen things? No, not really. I don’t think I’ve seen anything that wasn’t there.\n\nIt’s more like hearing stuff, like that old headset beep from work, or a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” and then I check and my laptop’s closed, there’s no phone ringing. But visually, no… I can’t think of anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found that you can get by with much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, my sleep is kind of all over the place sometimes, but feeling rested on less sleep? Not really something I’ve noticed.\n\nLike, if I only sleep a few hours, I usually just feel tired and kind of useless the next day. I wish I could get by on less sleep, honestly—my neighbor upstairs seems to vacuum at the worst times, which is… not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I get that “I barely slept and I’m totally fine” thing.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not completely falling apart or anything. I can still pay rent, keep food around, you know, keep a roof over my head. But it does mess with things.\n\nWork is the main one. I’ve kind of lost momentum with steady jobs because I miss deadlines, or I avoid calls when I’m supposed to be on them, and then people start treating me like I’m unreliable. Which… I guess I am, in those moments. It’s frustrating because customer service is already one of those jobs where one missed thing turns into three, and then you’re thinking about the whole queue and the little timer and—sorry, that’s not exactly the point.\n\nRelationships too, I guess. I see friends less. I don’t really make plans unless someone pushes me. And basic stuff slides, like meals, errands, even hiking, which I used to actually like. I can do it if I force myself, but it takes a lot more pushing than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I decided to stop liking things, it’s more like they just don’t… catch.\n\nI used to sketch a lot, and I’d keep these little coffee notes, like which beans tasted kind of chocolatey or burnt or whatever. And weekend hikes used to make me feel like myself. Lately I’ll open the notebook, or I’ll put my boots by the door, and I just kind of sit there. There’s nothing pulling me toward it.\n\nWhich is annoying, because the boots are right there, and I even bought new socks for hiking once—too expensive, honestly—but then I don’t go. It’s like the idea of it is still familiar, but the feeling isn’t really there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or irritable and your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I get irritated, sure, but I don’t know if it’s that kind of “extremely” irritated thing you mean.\n\nAnd happy… not really in a big way. My thoughts can feel busy sometimes, like I’ll be thinking about a bill, then a work message, then whether I forgot laundry in the washer — which I actually did once and had to rewash everything because it got that smell — but I don’t know if I’d call it racing.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your hallucinations, such as hearing voices, were particularly vivid or disturbing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it’s more noticeable than other times. Like I’ll hear the old headset beep, that little work call sound, even when my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing or anything.\n\nOr I’ll hear this supervisor-type voice say my name, or say “too slow,” which is… not great. It’s weird because I know the actual supervisor isn’t there, and half the time I’m not even working. It still kind of makes my stomach drop, like I’m behind on a queue again. My thoughts can feel busy sometimes, like I’ll be thinking about a bill, then a work message, then whether I forgot laundry in the washer — which I actually did once and had to rewash everything because it got that smell — but I don’t know if I’d call it racing.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your hallucinations, such as hearing voices, were particularly vivid or disturbing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it’s more noticeable than other times. Like I’ll hear the old headset beep, that little work call sound, even when my laptop’s closed and there’s no phone ringing or anything.\n\nOr I’ll hear this supervisor-type voice say my name, or say “too slow,” which is… not great. It’s weird because I know the actual supervisor isn’t there, and half the time I’m not even working. It still kind of makes my stomach drop, like I’m behind on a queue again. Customer service timers are honestly awful—sorry, I know that’s not the point.\n\nSo, yeah, it can be disturbing, mostly because it feels so specific. Like it’s using work stuff against me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're worthless or have a low opinion of yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I want to admit.\n\nIt’s usually over small stuff, like at work if I reply too slowly or I miss some little detail, I’ll keep replaying it after. Or with contract jobs I’ve done, I’ll think, okay, that one message took too long, so I must’ve let everyone down. And then my brain goes from that to, like, well, maybe I deserved to lose the job or get fired.\n\nWhich sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, because sometimes it’s just one email or one customer ticket, but it doesn’t feel small in my head. It turns into this whole… proof that I’m not really good enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely happy or extremely irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not for several days in a row.\n\nI mean, I can get irritable, like if something keeps beeping or there’s some customer email that’s worded in that passive-aggressive way—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But it doesn’t feel like this big several-day stretch where I’m just extremely happy or angry the whole time.\n\nExtremely happy, definitely not that I can remember. More like normal little moments, if anything. So… no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I get annoyed in the moment—like if there’s a weird work message or I’m already tired and something stupid happens with the system—but it usually doesn’t last days.\n\nI might complain about it too much, honestly. Like I’ll tell myself I’m just being “efficient” by noticing all the broken processes, but sometimes I’m just irritated. But several days in a row where I’m extremely irritable? I don’t think so. Nothing that stands out like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your hallucinations were so real that you acted on them as if they were real?\n\nPatient: Kind of… I mean, I’ve definitely reacted to it at first like it was real.\n\nLike I’ll hear the old headset beep, the one from work, or a supervisor-type voice saying my name or “too slow,” even when my laptop is closed and there’s no phone ringing. And I’ll sort of look around or check the laptop like, wait, did I miss something?\n\nIt’s weird because my brain goes into work mode immediately. Customer service stuff does that anyway—those little alert sounds get burned into your head. But then I realize, no, nothing’s actually on, so… I don’t know if that counts as “acting on it,” but I have responded to it like it was happening for a moment."
  }
